  • Donors. realism.split.excluded_roles = [development, fresh, earlier]: 306 development, 161 fresh and 31 earlier donors are excluded and 85 donors never touched by the realism work are held out, each with at least 3 replica views (min_replicas), up to 5 per donor (replicas_per_donor). The first split excluded only the development and fresh donors (117 held-out donors); the 31 earlier donors had appeared in the feature tables of earlier evaluation rounds and are given up for a strict set. Donor 0720700101 (no PPS products: no donor_background row and no exposure maps, so it cannot supply observing conditions) is excluded from the generation (runtime.exclude_donors of the preset) and has no replica views: it moves from held-out to ineligible, so the split is 306 development, 161 fresh, 31 earlier, 85 held-out and 55 ineligible donors (86 and 54 without the exclusion), split sha256 3f3104e4... (4296f161... without it). realism.frozen registers exclude_donors, n_heldout and split_sha256; mode: frozen refuses a run whose preset excludes other donors or whose split differs.

  • Observing conditions. donors.observing_conditions of the preset (see the review fixes below) is the one source of truth of the production generation and of instrument.replay.conditions; realism.frozen.conditions registers the value the preset must have: the evaluation is of what production generates. Registered in release 1.1 as unset, i.e. {rule: donor}; re-registered before any frozen run for release 1.1.1 as {rule: nn, n: 5, jitter: [exposure, roll, pointing]} (“Release 1.1.1” below).

  • Estimator. The vignetted + flat count matching above (realism.matching.ESTIMATOR); the classifiers, bootstrap and permutation settings of realism.frozen.c2st. mode: frozen refuses a run whose donor set, replicas, split, estimator, conditions or classifier settings differ (realism.report.frozen_mismatches).

  • Earlier look at the held-out donors. The evaluation helper computed count-matching factors, with the first (single-rate) estimator, for 564 views of the 117 donors of the first split: factors only, no features, no replay, no classifier. They are void: the estimator changed, and the strict set has 85 donors. The registered configuration is the one of the commit that carries this paragraph; the report records the commit and the config hash.

  • Run. slurm/realism_frozen.sh on one node after the independent review and the smoke test.

Review of release 1.1: the fixes (2026-10-24)¶

  • Observing conditions in the generation. donors.observing_conditions is a spec field, hashed only when set (None = the physics donor’s own, so no existing spec hash moves). With {rule: nn, n, jitter, table} the generation draws the conditions donor per view with conditions_donor.stage_conditions, the function the replay runs (the replica index comes from the targets row); the replay takes the preset’s value (instrument.replay.conditions: null), a changed value is a changed hash. The global observing_conditions is gone. Release 1.1 left it unset; release 1.1.1 sets it in the four presets.

  • Donor 0720700101 has no PPS products (no background row, no exposure maps) and cannot lend observing conditions. Excluding it is a rule of the GENERATION, not of the replica draw: campaigns/v4/targets_v4.csv and campaigns/v4_max/targets_v4_max.csv stay the pure draws (the replica reproduces under both the CLI and Kedro, no view id shifts), and the preset’s runtime.exclude_donors (obsid -> reason; the same for the four presets) is applied when the views are planned, to every targets list: campaign run, sim.plan_campaign, the mock-cutout and truth-sphere halo lists, check_donor_tables, campaign check, the realism selection and the donor split (the donor has no replicas, so it is never held out). An excluded view is not generated and is recorded, not dropped silently: status excluded: <reason> in the results CSV, excluded in the plan, and counted by check C1 (a target view of an excluded donor is expected to be absent). It is a runtime field because it changes no generated byte, so no spec or generation hash moves (the frozen registration stays valid); a donor is lifted from the list by editing the field, and the views are then generated (an excluded row never counts as done). 31 views of the maximal list (snapshots 67 and 72 of TNG-Cluster, 72 of TNG300) are affected. sim.plan_campaign and campaign run fail, before any view, with the list of target donors missing from a donor table the preset reads.

  • Environments merge key by key (merge_strategy: soft). A mapping is merged, so key: {} in an environment clears nothing; to clear one an environment writes key: null. tests/unit/test_kedro_environments.py holds this, refuses an empty-mapping override and lists what each environment changes.

  • Conditions donor. Under the rule nn the N_H recorded for a view (obs_meta.json, results CSV) is the one it was observed with (the conditions donor’s; the physics donor’s stays in the package and in conditions.json), and the plan-time check holds the whole conditions pool, not only the target donors, to the donor tables and the exposure maps. The replica index of the targets row enters the package only under nn (it picks the conditions donor), so the donor rule writes the obs_meta key set of golden_v1.1.

  • Jobs and shards. slurm/campaign_all.sh runs every snapshot it can, lists those campaign run refused or failed and ends with status 4 (5: only a check failed). A donor-table shard is given as k/n (--params ...shard=k/n; Kedro splits --params on commas).

  • Frozen mode registers the sha256 of params:realism (less paths and process counts), the preset and its spec hash, and compares them and the replay record (count-matching table set, agn on, no re-seed, no replay options) at the start of the selection and of the count matching and again before the report. The registered conditions are compared with the SPEC’s.

  • Count matching chooses the background region rule on the donor’s image and fits the replica over the same region in R500 units; a donor region the replica cannot fill is recorded (no-mock-background-region). This changes the estimator: the factors of the development tests above were made with a rule per side and are not those of the registered estimator (region: donor-rule).

  • Fixtures are versioned with the package: release 1.1 reads golden_v1.1/, golden_v1.1_fits/, golden_v1.1.SHA256SUMS; release 1.0.1 keeps golden_v1/; manifest.json lists both. Main’s own check_fixtures rejects a header-only CSV, which both golden trees hold (checks/campaign_check_issues.csv); the branch’s accepts it.

Release 1.1.1 (2026-10-25): production observes under the conditions donor¶

Author’s decision: the observing conditions of every generated view come from the N = 5 nearest real observations (nn), not from the view’s own physics donor (donor). The four v1 presets (v1, v1_tng300, v1_tng100, v1_tng50) set donors.observing_conditions = {rule: nn, n: 5, jitter: [exposure, roll, pointing]} (config.PRODUCTION_CONDITIONS; the field default stays None because a dataclass cannot hold a mapping default, so CampaignSpec() equals v1 but for this one field, and a test says so). It is the one hashed spec field that drives the generation, the replay (instrument.replay.conditions: null) and the frozen evaluation (realism.frozen.conditions registers {rule: nn, n: 5, jitter: [exposure, roll, pointing]}). The jitter terms are the archive jitter already implemented (conditions_donor.draw_jitter: every number is drawn whatever the list holds, so the order of the list changes only the hash; the canonical order of JITTER_KINDS is used). Nothing else changes: the physics donor still fixes the redshift, the target list, R500 and the comparison target of the realism evaluation.

Why. With donor, the K = 5 replicas of one reference observation are five copies of one observation: same exposure, same background levels, same footprint, same roll. A classifier can read the donor off the data-quality features, and a machine-learning model trained on the mocks (claim D of the paper plan) then learns the donor’s observing conditions as an identity, not the cluster. Under nn the five replicas of a donor are observed under different real observations of similar conditions, so they are not deterministic copies of one observation. The evidence (96 physics donors x 5 replicas, snapshots 67-99 all full; INDIST/cond/node/analysis_n5.txt; 171 distinct conditions donors among the 480 views, replicas of one donor with distinct conditions: 1.00):

donor

nn (N = 5)

chance

supervised physics-donor accuracy, data-quality features

0.81

0.09

0.01

supervised physics-donor accuracy, all features

0.73

0.17

0.01

replica intraclass correlation ICC(1), data-quality features

0.82

0.33

replica intraclass correlation ICC(1), all features

0.58

0.32

real-vs-mock C2ST (logistic, 194 + 194 views, one replica per donor, grouped by donor)

0.879

0.883

0.5

real-vs-mock C2ST (boosting)

0.961

0.970

0.5

The identity of the donor falls to near chance and the replicas are no longer copies, while the realism against the real archive does not change (0.879 -> 0.883; the classifier separates the mocks from the real data equally well under either rule, so the rule costs no realism). The ICC of the features that depend on the cluster (log_net_r500 0.87 -> 0.80, log_s_over_b 0.73 -> 0.64) stays high; that of the background (log_bkg_rate 0.99 -> 0.39, hr_bkg 0.98 -> 0.10, cam_ratio_bkg 0.99 -> 0.33, fov_frac 1.00 -> 0.29) falls: what is shared between replicas is the cluster, not the observation.

Consequences, checked on the code.

  • Spec hashes (v1 b78d7ff176a9bc6a, v1_tng300 ebab01e28523ff74, v1_tng100 7fec18d41f3cda16, v1_tng50 eadae5c7f9d987e0) and every tree generated before: a release 1.1.0 tree fails campaign check on the spec hash, by design. realism.frozen is re-registered with the new conditions and spec hash before any frozen run; config_sha256 is unchanged (the frozen block is outside that hash) and tests/unit/test_realism_frozen.py recomputes the registration from conf/base.

  • Conditions pool: 588 of the 604 donors of donor_conditions (577 of 593 when the n = 5 evidence was made; see the last paragraph) (a background row with finite live times for every camera, a usable core offset, an active-CCD row). Donor 0720700101 (no PPS products) is outside the pool; it is also in runtime.exclude_donors, so it is neither generated nor drawn.

  • check_donor_tables under nn holds the whole pool, not only the targets, to the background, nh_total, soft_ccd, mos_background tables and to the PPS exposure maps (a conditions donor is observed through its own maps).

  • Count matching (realism.matching): f = N_target / N_mock in the donor’s annulus. The replica is the stored view, so under nn its counts follow the conditions donor’s exposure and background, and the target is the physics donor’s real counts scaled to the products’ effective live time (flux held fixed; “Review of release 1.1.1” below corrects the first version of this item, f = N_physics / N_mock). The physics donor stays the comparison target (donor_package.obsid is the physics donor in obs_meta.json). The replay, drawing from the same conditions stream, observes the same conditions donor as the generation and applies f to the cluster photons. Tested in tests/unit/test_realism_matching_conditions.py.

  • Golden fixtures regenerated as golden_v1.1.1 (the 1.1 products carry the old spec hash and the donor’s own conditions); those of 1.0.1 and 1.1.0 stay beside them.

Conditions table: rule-based pool (author’s decision, 2026-10-25). donor_conditions.csv was built from the replica target list only (593 donors, 577 in the pool); the maximal list (campaigns/v4_max/targets_v4_max.csv, 604 donors) has 11 more, and under nn a physics donor needs its own row, so plan_campaign and campaign run refused the maximal list. The node donor_tables.build_donor_conditions now takes both lists (replica rows first), and the table was rebuilt through the Kedro pipeline and shipped with its SHA256SUMS line: 604 rows, the 593 old rows unchanged, sha256 05839db907c38329d419680c8e4d783edd162272b84593dbab5d9e92f0a42bb2 (registered again on 2026-11-03 as 30a8f3ec4cf6551852850ad900af68296651b7d974c2f6f919ebea8ebb85f6de after the rebuild at the final centres, before any frozen run; see “Independent review, round 5”). The pool rule is the same as before (a background row with finite live times for every camera, a usable core offset, an active-CCD row), no special case: all 11 added donors (0303950101, 0650384601, 0763670201, 0803050101, 0803050701, 0821290601, 0862640301, 0914590301, 0914590501, 0914590701, 0914591201; 138 of the 9615 views of the maximal list) satisfy it, so the pool is 588 donors. The N = 5 evidence above came from the 577-donor pool; the 588 pool follows the same rule, and the nearest-5 sets of 138 of the 593 replica-list donors change because an added donor is among their neighbours. The golden fixture views are not affected (their candidates are the same under both tables). realism.frozen.conditions_table_sha256 registers the table, and mode: frozen refuses another pool (tests/unit/test_realism_frozen.py). The previous table is kept at tngxmm_dev/V111/tables_before/. check_donor_tables (plan_campaign, campaign run) passes for the 604 donors of both lists under all four presets.

Review of release 1.1.1: the fixes (2026-10-25, before any frozen run)¶

The review of 17a175b found a sizing error in the generation and several descriptions of the wrong donor. All fixed in the release; the golden products were regenerated.

Photon list sized for the conditions donor (F1). generate_view sized the list with max(physics donor cameras_s); the instrument then used the conditions donor’s (jittered) live times. The keep probability of a camera is p = ARF / A_gen x t_cam / t_gen, so a conditions camera longer than exp_time makes p exceed ARF / A_gen and the summed probability could exceed 1: the stage printed a warning and fell back to independent thinning (photons reused across cameras). Measured on the 12455 target rows of both lists, without the exposure jitter, a conditions camera was longer than the list in 46 % of the (row, candidate) pairs, the summed probability exceeded 1 in 2.4 %, by up to 4.5 x (REVIEW_V111/texp_ratio*.out). Now the observing conditions are drawn first (conditions_donor.draw_conditions: the conditions donor, its jitter, the rolled position angle, from the view’s own conditions stream), the list is sized with longest_exposure_s of the jittered live times, and stage_events thins and retargets the absorbed list afterwards, continuing the same stream (the replay calls both halves in stage_conditions). The summed probability above 1 now RAISES (simulate_instrument.check_camera_partition; the photon-reuse fallback is removed), so it cannot pass unnoticed. Under the rule donor nothing changes (the list is sized for the physics donor’s longest time). A replay of a stored view under conditions with a longer camera than the stored list holds (an ablation arm drawn from other conditions) raises in the same way.

Stream consequences. The seeds (the sha256-v1 keys) and the set of purposes are unchanged; a new purpose or key is not introduced. Which streams change in value, under nn: (1) photon_counts, photon_energy, photon_position and absorption: exp_time changes with the list, so the Poisson counts of every particle, hence the whole sequence, change (views whose conditions donor has the same longest time as the physics donor are the exception, none in practice); (2) the streams consumed per photon or per event, conditions (the keep draw random(len(e)), the first values are those of the old list), partition (random(len(eobs))) and the per-camera psf, rmf, oot, background_qpb, agn_counts, background_sky streams, whose consumption follows the number of photons and events. Unchanged in value: the conditions donor, its jitter, the permutation of the candidates (conditions before the keep draw; the donor-level permutation seed), the posangle draw (now made before the photons, a stream of its own), donor, z_obs, dither, agn_sky, global_legacy. rng_fingerprint of every view therefore changes. The golden products had to be regenerated; so does any tree made before this change.

Row and obs_meta describe the products (F2). exposure_pn/mos1/mos2_s, filter, pn_mode, nh_1e22 and posangle_deg of the results row and of obs_meta.json are those the instrument was run with (the conditions donor’s jittered live times, filter, PN mode and N_H, the rolled position angle). posangle_deg had recorded the angle drawn before the conditions while the products used the rolled one. The physics donor’s values stay under physics_exposure_*_s, physics_filter, physics_pn_mode, physics_nh_1e22 (the N_H the STORED photon list events_v*_abs.h5 is absorbed with, and the one the retarget starts from) and, in obs_meta only, physics_posangle_deg; conditions_obsid names the observation of the products. obs_meta.nh_1e22 is therefore the products’ N_H (the conditions donor’s); the absorbed list carries physics_nh_1e22; stage_events.h5 is absorbed with the conditions donor’s. obs_meta.exposure_s holds the per-camera times the instrument used; donor_package stays the physics donor’s package (the replay re-derives the conditions from it). Readers changed: the replay of the physics donor’s own conditions starts from physics_nh_1e22 and physics_posangle_deg; the mock photometry takes the products’ live times (view_meta._exposure_s); the legacy box-frame source projection reads the products’ roll; the mask flux limit is the conditions donor’s (donor_of_view: its AGN normalisation made the view’s sources); bank keeps donor_id the physics donor (the grouping key of the donor folds: the donor of the cluster, not of the data quality) and the ML lint forbids conditions_obsid and physics_*. The gate P of the donor population compares the targets table, i.e. the physics donors, and is unaffected.

Count matching holds the physical flux fixed (F6, decided by the author). Under nn the target counts are the physics donor’s real net counts scaled to the observing time of the replica: N_target = N_physics,real x t_cond / t_phys, with t the ARF-weighted effective live time (MOS1 395, MOS2 400, PN 1259 cm2; conditions_donor.effective_exposure_s) of the products (obs_meta.exposure_s, the jittered times) and of the physics donor’s package; f = N_target / N_mock, capped at 1. The data quality then follows the conditions donor while the flux stays the physics donor’s. Before, f = N_physics / N_mock made a replica observed longer than the physics donor too faint a cluster (thinned to the physics donor’s counts) and a shorter one too bright. The ratio is 1 under the rule donor and for trees without exposure_s, so f is unchanged there; a tree with a conditions record and no exposure_s is refused. Approximation: only the live times scale the target; the differences of filter, vignetting and footprint between the two donors are not in N_target, so f absorbs them. realism.matching.ESTIMATOR (and the registration realism.frozen.count_matching) now carries target: physics-flux-at-products-exposure and the weights, re-registered before any frozen run; the count-matching table has the columns exposure_ratio and net_target.

Frozen mode and the conditions table (F3). The replays’ conditions are compared with the registered ones including the sha256 of the resolved condition table and the absence of distance weights (also for the spec). The table sha256 is a raw hash of the file’s bytes: the same table re-saved with other line endings or number formats is another table for the registration.

Campaign checks (F4, F8). C2 expects the products of the conditions donor’s cameras (conditions.json chosen in the condition table), not the physics donor’s package; C4’s message under nn names the conditions record.

e2e helpers (F5). The instrument e2e helpers replay a stored view through its conditions (_v1view.conditions), not the physics package with the conditions N_H. The Thin1-versus-Thick soft-band test had a threshold of 1.2 on a ratio that is 1.17-1.25 over draws of the same view (1.207 in v1.1.0, 1.17 on the 1.1.1 draw); it now compares the ratio r with its Poisson error sigma = r sqrt(1/n_thin + 1/n_thick) (an upper bound on the scatter of the correlated runs): r > 1 + 3 sigma in 0.2-0.5 keV, n_thin - n_thick > sqrt(n_thin + n_thick) in 0.5-2 keV (the effect is 6-9 %), and r below the pure CCF transmission ratio of the photon list plus 3 sigma (the background does not depend on the filter and dilutes the ratio).

Site (F7). runtime_env.sh massfit selects the site file like slurm/lib.sh and tngxmm.core.site; TNGXMM_MBPROJ_RESP is documented in the environment and site READMEs and set in laptop.env.example.

Review of release 1.1.1, round 2 (2026-10-25, before any frozen run)¶

The second review of 889671a (17a175b..889671a) found two defects of the first fixes and one leak of the design; the author decided the third. Measurements: tngxmm_dev/REVIEW_V111/round2/ (phys_time.out, fold_cross.out, replay_mismatch.out) and tngxmm_dev/V111/fix2/fold_cost.out.

The count-matching ratio uses the REAL exposure of the physics donor (NEW-1). exposure_ratio divided by donor_package.cameras_s, which for a replica is the target row’s JITTERED live times (exposure_jittered is True on all 2840 + 9615 rows; the jittered effective time over the real one spans 0.58-1.73, 16-84 % 0.86-1.17), while N_physics,real was counted on the real observation, at the real exposure. The ratio was therefore not t_cond / t_phys,real but t_cond / (t_phys,real x jitter), a noise of about 17 % on the target of every view; the golden view with its physics donor as conditions donor had a ratio of 0.975, not 1. Now the physics package carries cameras_real_s (the exposure_<cam>_donor_s columns of the targets row; draw_donor keeps the pre-jitter times), exposure_ratio uses it with the same ARF weights as t_cond, and a jittered package without real times raises (a tree of the first 1.1.1 must be regenerated). What the jitter means here: the replica’s target row jitters the physics donor’s times, but under nn the instrument is run with the CONDITIONS donor’s (separately jittered) times and the target is a real count at the real exposure, so the physics jitter never enters; the only jitter in the ratio is the conditions donor’s own exposure_scale in the numerator. With the conditions donor equal to the physics donor and no exposure jitter the ratio is exactly 1 (unit test); the golden view 0 (self donor, exposure_scale 1.0 after the clip to the candidates’ range) has ratio 1.0000. physics_exposure_*_s (results row, schema, INTERFACES) now hold the physics donor’s REAL live times, as the name says (they held the jittered times of the target row). Re-registered: realism.matching.ESTIMATOR and realism.frozen.count_matching carry physics_exposure: real.

A replay under other conditions no longer aborts the node (NEW-2). replay_view staged the conditions with the realisation stream (inst), so reseed redrew the exposure jitter, and a {rule: donor} replay of an nn tree used the physics donor’s jittered times against a list sized for the conditions donor’s: check_camera_partition raised, with no catch per view (rule-donor replay of an nn tree: 6.2 % of the (row, candidate) pairs; reseed: 2.8 %). Now (1) the conditions are drawn from the view’s OWN streams (realisation 0, the generation’s), so a re-seed redraws the instrument only and the conditions donor, its jitter, its roll and pointing are reproduced exactly (conditions_seed of replay.json is the generation’s); (2) before anything is staged the longest camera time of the conditions is compared with the exp_time of the stored list, and a view that asks for more is REFUSED (ConditionsRefused, status refused: conditions longer than the stored photon list): instrument.replay_views records it for that view (n_refused; no merged, so the realism features skip it) and goes on, the certify stage lists it in stage/<variant>_refused.json and leaves it out of the variant’s targets. A rule-donor replay (or any replay under conditions other than the generation’s) needs a donor-rule tree, whose list is sized for the physics donor; on an nn tree it is refused for the views whose conditions donor is longer. Frozen mode requires reseed: null and the preset’s conditions, so it is unaffected.

The conditions donor stays in the physics donor’s donor fold (NEW-3, decided by the author). The conditions donor lends the data quality (footprint, background, live times, pointing, roll, filters) to the mock; the condition space includes log theta500 and the core offset, so it identifies the observation. With the 5 nearest of ALL 588 pool observations, 1785 of 2769 replica views (64.5 %) used a conditions donor in another donor fold than the physics donor, and 513 of the 584 conditions donors served physics donors of more than one fold: a mock of fold f carried the data quality of a real observation of fold g, so the model trained without fold g saw it through the mocks of the other folds. That breaks the held-out integrity of claim D (the folds exist so that no real cluster is predicted by a model whose training mocks borrowed its conditions). Now the conditions rule has fold_restricted: true (hashed in donors.observing_conditions, set in the four v1 presets; false/unset is available for a study only): the candidates of a physics donor are the pool observations of ITS donor fold, n nearest in the unchanged condition space (the standardising spreads stay those of the whole pool). The folds are campaigns/v4/donor_folds.csv (the package’s one assignment, identical to donor_fold of both target lists and to the bank’s folds; shipped in the wheel; realism.frozen.donor_folds_sha256 registers it, the replays record the digest they used). The conditions table and its sha256 are unchanged. The record of conditions.json has fold_restricted and fold. Pool per fold (pool 588): 123 / 113 / 115 / 114 / 123 (all 604 table donors 126 / 116 / 116 / 120 / 126): every fold holds at least 113 candidates for n = 5, 22 times n. Cost (604 physics donors, the 4 non-self candidates, distance in the standardised units of the condition space): the candidate distance median 1.76 -> 2.52, 16-84 % 1.15-2.82 -> 1.75-3.60, 95 % 3.96 -> 4.59, maximum 6.31 -> 7.11; the nearest neighbour 1.42 -> 2.03 (median); the mean of the four 1.75 -> 2.45 (a factor 1.37 in the median per donor); per fold the median rises from 1.67-1.80 to 2.44-2.61. The candidate list changes for all 604 donors, and the share of non-self candidates with another PN filter, MOS filter or PN submode (the categorical penalty) doubles, 10.8 % -> 22.6 %. These neighbours are further away and less often of the same filter; that is the price of the integrity, accepted by the author. After: 0 of 2840 replica views have a conditions donor in another fold and 0 of the 587 conditions donors serve more than one fold. The diagnostic bank.assign_donor_folds.check_conditions_donor_folds (run by the node bank.build_manifest, which raises on a nonzero count; bank metadata conditions_donor_id) verifies it on a generated tree (expected 0 and 0). The spec hashes change (v1 198b80e9d4563eec, v1_tng300 ea44c41bd7505585, v1_tng100 8c67f0845616ba25, v1_tng50 1af83cda12b0cc4b), so does every conditions draw, and the golden was regenerated. Found on the way, fixed: 15 physics donors of the target lists (70 replica views, 291 of the maximal list; not 0720700101) are outside the pool with a NaN in the condition space (a camera without live time), and condition_distances gave them NO candidates (NaN distances), so their views failed with “no conditions candidate” although this section’s predecessor states that such a donor “still draws its neighbours from the pool”. A physics donor now leaves the dimensions it lacks out of its own distances (tests/unit/test_instrument_conditions_donor.py). The “before” numbers above include this for those donors.

Minor. The replays record conditions_table_sha256 when they run and frozen mode compares that record (it recomputed the digest of the file at report time, which may have changed since); the same for donor_folds_sha256. reimage_mock.partition_to_cameras raises on a camera probability sum above 1 like the instrument (the u = None fallback that drew the cameras independently is gone). runtime_env.sh unsets its site-lookup helper. The 0.5-2 keV check of the Thin1-versus-Thick test is a 3 sigma margin of the count difference (it was one).

Review of release 1.1.1, round 3 (2026-10-25, before any frozen run)¶

R3-1 (major, latent). realism.pipeline.replay_directories keeps the replay records that ran (those with merged); a view refused per view (round 2: conditions longer than the stored photon list) therefore dropped out of the evaluation without a trace, and the report did not state the count. Frozen mode now requires replays.n_refused == 0 (absent = 0) and the report’s replay section carries n_refused, conditions_table_sha256 and donor_folds_sha256. A study that wants refused views may run an exploratory mode; the frozen evaluation takes every replay. The check is on the record, not on the registration, so no config_sha256 changes. R3-2. The replay redraws the conditions donor from the view’s own streams; for a tree made under other conditions (before the fold-restricted rule) and replayed under the preset’s it drew another donor than the one the stored products carry, silently. With the preset’s own conditions (rule, n, jitter, fold restriction, weights) and no re-seed the redrawn donor must equal obs_meta.conditions_obsid, else that view is refused with its own status (refused: stored conditions donor differs, conditions_donor.StoredConditionsDiffer, a ConditionsRefused, so it is recorded and counted like the other refusal). A tree without the key (earlier releases) and a rule-donor draw are not checked; a study’s own conditions or a re-seed are not held to the stored donor. R3-3. bank.build_manifest_node raising on a conditions donor of another fold is under test (the fixtures run attach: false). R3-4. The manifest keeps conditions_donor_id (string dtype through the CSVs; listed as an identity column of the ML-view lint, so the ML view drops it; INTERFACES 4.4). The conditions record carries dims_skipped, the condition dimensions the physics donor lacks. A physics donor lacking a category (a camera without a filter: the string ‘nan’) mismatched every pool row, adding the same penalty to all its distances: ranking unaffected, the recorded distance inflated; the category is now left out like a missing continuous dimension. Draws are unchanged (the distances differ by a constant per physics donor), so no product, spec hash or golden changes; only the distance of the 15 donors concerned in conditions.json is smaller.

Release 1.1.2 (2026-10-26): the code issues of the docstring audit¶

A fix release. The docstring audit (every public function documented against its code) found places (numbered 1-42) where the code and its description disagreed; the list is ranked in tngxmm_dev/DOCS/CODE_MISMATCHES.md (numbers below are its numbers). Each item was checked against the code, fixed with a test, or left alone with a reason. No spec hash, no generation hash and no production image, table or fit moves: the defaults stay bit-identical (the registered hashes of the four v1 presets and realism.frozen are unchanged, and tests/unit/test_config.py still pins them).

Fixed.

  • IDs (1, 2). schemas.ids.make_mock_obsid takes style='v4' (the production 13-character fixed-width {snap:03d}{halo:08d}v{view}[r<k>], now the default) or style='legacy' (the 11-character v1.0 form); ViewKey.mock_obsid is the v4 form and ViewKey.legacy_mock_obsid the legacy one; donors.build_replica_targets.make_mock_obsid_v4 calls the same function; is_mock_obsid accepts both; schemas.products.PERCAM_RE accepts the 13-character obsid in a per-camera product name (the pipeline code already read it through campaign_checks._PERCAM_RE). Nothing in src called the old helpers, so no product changes.

  • Seed offset (3): the ledger is corrected, the seeds are not. seed_offset enters no seed; the streams are keyed by sim.rng_campaign, the simulation, snapshot, halo, projection, replica, purpose and camera (core.rng.seed_for). Making the offset enter the seeds would change every random draw, hence every product ever generated and every pairing with the V4 views, for a label that has no scientific use. So the ledger entry run.random_streams (and docs/dev/INTERFACES.md) now say what the code does. The field is kept, not deprecated: it is a hashed spec field recorded in obs_meta.json, the results rows, the bank manifest and campaign check C3, so removing it would move every spec hash. It is a provenance label (a different value is a different campaign hash with identical draws), and a test (test_seed_offset_is_recorded_but_enters_no_seed) pins that.

  • Truth provenance text (7). truth_sphere.selection_text named the mock phase “the FoF members inside the sphere” whatever the loader; with the production loader ‘cutout’ the mock cells are every gas cell of the sphere. The text now follows the loader (fof, cutout, or the named selection). The sentence is written into each truth file (particle_selection); truth files written before 1.1.2 keep the old wording and are not rewritten (their numbers are right, only the sentence is imprecise; the new files differ from the old in that one attribute).

  • Photometry (16, 17). The ‘rejected’ branch of find_peak_centre_guarded cannot fire at the default max_move_theta500 = 1, because the search radius is already that limit. Making it meaningful (a default below 1) would move the centres of the real fits and of every mock fit, so the default stays and the documentation is honest: the guard is inert in production, the centre flag is ok, edge or empty, and the rejected counts of a production run are 0 (config mock_centre doc, fit_qc, build_realfit_targets; a test pins both the inert default and the rejection below 1). campaign_lx(spec=None) used to raise AttributeError (pipeline._preset_spec returns None for an unknown preset); it now measures with the production preset v1.

  • Instrument (20, 21). The fov_map cache was keyed by the ARF array only, so a response with a shifted vignetting centre (vig_axis = 'ccf' builds one by dataclasses.replace) could get its parent’s map; the key now carries the theta map and the axis. Under vig_axis = 'pointing' the response is the parent and the key is as before. The reimage_mock header said FILTER='Thin1' while the Medium ARF was used; it now records the filter of the response in use (Medium by default). That tool is a method test, not a production stage.

  • Targets and folds (15, 40). core.pool.read_targets filled the donor of each view (obs_meta.json key z_donor_obsid) only from a legacy donor_obsid column; the replica tables use donor_id. It reads either. The only value that changes is that provenance key in newly generated replica views (it was null); no image, table or hash depends on it. For assign_donor_folds without links: the links are not needed for the fold integrity of claim D. A replica view has one donor (its redshift donor is that donor; replica tables have no z_donor_id), so a link joins no groups (test_replica_views_add_no_link_so_the_donors_stage_needs_none: the folds are identical with and without), and the conditions donor of the nn rule is drawn from the physics donor’s fold (fold_restricted, release 1.1.1; tests in test_conditions_fold_restricted.py). Passing the links could only change folds that are committed (campaigns/v4/donor_folds.csv), so the donors stage keeps calling it without and the docstrings say why.

  • Small items. Help text of campaign mock-cutout --tree (9: the tree is the campaign tree the views read, not “a new tree”); the sph_map ImportError names the right module (10); data/manifest.json no longer advertises a tngxmm data pull that does not exist (11); sas-queue run --stage-only prints the real number of finished entries (22) and its _with_cpus call, which changed nothing, is gone (25); closure_grid.fit_done compares the whole fit_config (it ignored the preset, crop cap, pixel size and the seed-photometry keys) and closure_grid fit exits 1 when a fit failed (37); slurm/closure_grid.sh said 216 views for the 108 of the one generator arm; sim.rng_campaign = None falls back to the preset name in the generation as everywhere else, not the stream key ‘None’ (5); sim.t_sampling = 'round_up' is refused when the options are built, not at the first photon (6); compute_true_profiles.mock_matched_kwargs (unused, returned a key its function rejects) is removed (8); default_dependencies no longer binds a draw_donor that fails on call with a TypeError, and a view without a donor package raises a clear error (18); the validation gate report and sample-matched --scan append their extensions, so a prefix with a dot keeps it (39); the fit_hydrostatic --dry-run listing no longer repeats jobs (35); pps_point_sources does not download into the production cache by default (34); measure_apertures rescales the centre only when it resamples the image (27, an up-to-1 per cent offset for a pixel scale within the 0.05 arcsec tolerance; identical for the 4 arcsec grids of production), and prepare_lx_image carries an explicit centre or the header’s CLUSTX/CLUSTY to the resampled grid (28; also identical at 4 arcsec).

Left alone, with the reason.

  • 19 (read_source_list replaces FLUX and FLUX_ERR of every source): documented and deliberate, the PPS/catalogue EPIC flux convention; changing it changes the masks.

  • 4 (RuntimeSpec.fit_workers unused): a runtime field, in no hash; its documentation already says it is not read.

  • 12 (TNGXMM_MEM_PROBE adds a memory block without a budget): documented in core.membudget, intended for diagnosis.

  • 13 (_default_mu always the production mu_tvir): documented; the only preset family has one mu_tvir.

  • 14 (apply_absorption has its own TBabs table): the table is the one of the mock; replacing it changes every absorbed spectrum.

  • 23 (pointing radii clipped onto the cap circle): a design choice of the jitter; redrawing would change every conditions draw. The comment now says “clipped”.

  • 24 (PN_ROWS unused), 26, 30, 31, 32, 36, 38, 41, 42 (unused constants, a half-pixel centre convention, stale comments, the 2018/2020 year): trivial, no behaviour. The ORIGIN header of the ARFs: changing it changes the data-package checksums.

  • 29 (measure_fixed_apertures with an invalid background gives NaN in every aperture): the same rule as measure_apertures (no background, no luminosity); not a bug.

  • 33 (patch_apec applied twice leaves the APEC root and version tag inconsistent): needs mbproj2d to reproduce, which the login-node environment lacks; a blind change could invalidate the rate caches of the fits.

The soft-band luminosity excess of TNG-Cluster (2026-10-26)¶

Finding. At fixed true M500c (5e14, z = 0.2) the soft-band luminosity of TNG-Cluster is +0.28 dex above the observed relations (hydrostatic bias b = 0). By reference: Bulbul+19 +0.41 dex, Lovisari+20 +0.28, Pratt+09 +0.20 (the spread of the references is itself +-0.1 dex). Each 0.1 of hydrostatic bias adds about +0.09 dex. Source: the author’s analysis in tngxmm_dev/LX/ and tngxmm_dev/LX/followup/.

Explained: +0.19 +- 0.06 (stat) +- 0.07 (weak-lensing mass-scale systematic), about 70 per cent. The excess is in the emission measure:

term

dex

note

gas fraction squared

+0.04 +- 0.03

against weak-lensing f_gas: TNG 0.140, observed 0.134 in the TNG cosmology

clumping

+0.054

consistent with the observed sqrt(C) = 1.07 +- 0.05 at R500

density-profile shape

+0.08 +- 0.06

concentrated inside 0.5 R500

cool-core selection mix

+0.02 +- 0.03

Remainder: +0.09 +- 0.09 dex, consistent with zero but not proven closed; it sits within the spread between the references and the mass-calibration systematics.

Ruled out. Subhalo-bound gas: 0.001 dex. Temperature and metallicity: about 0. The pipeline: its intrinsic luminosity matches temet’s own X-ray luminosity of the same halos to +0.0095 dex (median pipeline / temet ratio after the (1+z) band correction, sd 0.026 dex, 2463 halos of snapshots 40-99; the per-snapshot medians are not flat, +0.003 dex at snapshot 99 to +0.06 dex at snapshot 40, so +0.0095 is the pooled figure; the trend follows the gas temperature and phase structure, not redshift: regressing on log T_sl, log(T_ew/T_sl) and log Z_ew leaves per-snapshot medians within ±0.004 dex, both emission tables being exactly Λ_rest/(1+z)). The cutout gas geometry (box cutout against FoF members, 30 halos) changes L by a median +0.001 dex, at most 0.004 dex at R500 and 0.010 dex at 1.5 Mpc. The photometry, the instrument and the masks are downstream of these comparisons. Population values at 5e14 (log L, f_gas, clumping, T, Z) are in tngxmm_dev/LX/pop_trends.csv.

Decision (author). The excess is reported as a property of TNG-Cluster and is not calibrated away: no luminosity factor, no change of the gas, no calibration to an observed relation. The matching of mocks to real clusters stays on the TRUE mass (the catalogue M500c of the mock against the reference mass of the observation) together with the count matching of the realism evaluation (the counts of a view are matched to those of its donor at fixed flux, “Count matching” in “Realism, release 1.1”); a mock is not given another mass to look less bright.

Release 1.2 (planned, 2026-10-26)¶

Since 2026-11-25 (“Pass 2 with six projections and the added observations”): the six icosahedral views are implemented as pass 2 (sim.projection = 'icosahedral6' in the presets of config.PASS2_PRESETS).

Not implemented; recorded so that the log says what comes next (the author’s plan, after release 1.1.1). Release 1.2 changes the number and the geometry of the views of a halo:

  • 6 icosahedral-vertex projections per halo instead of the 3 axis-aligned views (VIEWS = (0, 1, 2)), for all halos: the lines of sight are the 6 axes through opposite vertices of the icosahedron (equivalently through the face centres of the dodecahedron). The two directions of one axis give the same projected image of optically thin gas, so 6 axes are 6 distinct views. The axes are not the box axes;

  • each projection has its own conditions donor (the nn rule of release 1.1.1 drawn per projection, fold-restricted as now), so the six views of a halo are not six looks through one set of observing conditions; physics donors are distinct up to the available pool, with the shortfall reported (design note of 2026-10-27);

  • the gas is cut as a cube of half-side 4 sqrt(2) R500c (about 5.66 R500c; “5.7” in the first version of this plan is the rounded value). Each view integrates a cylinder of radius 4 R500c and half-length 4 R500c along its axis. Every point of such a cylinder is within sqrt(4^2 + 4^2) = 4 sqrt(2) R500c of the centre, whatever the axis, and the cube of half-side 4 sqrt(2) R500c contains that sphere, so one cube serves all six axes. The cubes exist (job 1889862); the generation needs a cylinder-size parameter separate from the cube half-side (cylinder 4 R500c for all halos);

  • folds and the bootstrap are grouped by halo: the six projections of a halo share a fold and are resampled together.

The new spec fields and their hashes are written with the release, not here.

Release 1.1.3 (unreleased): the review of the documentation¶

Since then: 1.1.3 is the version of the package (pyproject.toml). The six open decisions below were taken in “Release 1.1.3, continued: the node work (2026-10-27)”; the cluster PSF (decision 3) was changed again on 2026-11-03 (“Independent review of the replacements, round 1”, item C).

The documentation of release 1.1.2 (the Sphinx site and the Kedro-Viz graph only) was read independently of the code and the development records. The read gave 53 findings, 12 of them major: among these, a closure test that fails with a realistic background while the page said it passed, a realism claim the measured AUCs do not support, and a replica list that fails gate P. Every finding is answered in REVIEW_RESPONSE_v1.1.3.md. This section records what was measured and decided in answering it. No spec hash, generation hash, golden product or production table changes.

Measured on the login node (existing tables only).

  • Snapshot against observed redshift (ledger method.snapshot_to_zobs). Replica list: |z_obs - z_snap| median 0.033, p90 0.088, 432 views outside the +-0.15 window. The 9,774 views of the v1.0.1 trees (maximal and box lists): |dz| median 0.055, p90 0.119; ln[M500c(500 rho_c(z_obs)) / M500c(500 rho_c(z_snap))] of the same particles median +0.001, 16-84 per cent -0.036 / +0.042, 5-95 per cent -0.062 / +0.073, negative when z_obs > z_snap.

  • Sensitivity test S7, the (1-b) of the matching mass (internal/ledger_tests/matching_bias_s7.py, SENSITIVITY_TESTS R12): gate P fails at (1-b)_PSZ2 = 0.78, 0.84 and 0.90 (mass D 0.241, 0.229, 0.207); the median halo mass of the replica views moves by -0.008 and -0.018 dex; 8.5 and 5.6 per cent of the production views are drawn again.

  • QC exclusion of the 43 D1 mock fits (v4s1): the median ln(M_X / M_HSE) at R500c is -0.166 with every fit and -0.072 without the 12 flagged ones; M_X / M_true 0.825 and 0.835; M_HSE / M_true 0.866 and 0.881. The QC selection moves the method bias by about 8 per cent, so results are given with and without it.

  • Donor core offsets (donor_core_offsets.csv): median 1.7 arcmin, p90 2.9, cap 6 (ledger instrument.cluster_psf_onaxis).

  • Intrinsic hydrostatic bias of the v1.0.1 population: median M_HSE(< R500c) / M500c 0.883 (TNG-Cluster, 2,460 halo-snapshots) and 0.914 (TNG300, 742).

Documentation. New pages: glossary, science definitions, validation status, samples and selection, figures (nine figures from existing results, tngxmm.viz.doc_figures, inputs recorded by root, path and sha256). The development records moved to a “Development log” section. Corrected: PIPELINE section 7 (the closure rule is not met), the gas selection of the presets and of the ledger (the 4 R500c cube, not the FoF group), the AGN counts (soft band in production), the MOS RGA vignetting (measured on the donors), the abundance citation (lodders2003), references to removed presets, the count matching (realism replays only; production mocks are not thinned). New ledger entries: instrument.sky_background (open), instrument.cluster_psf_onaxis (open), method.snapshot_to_zobs. A test compares every field = value of a ledger value line with the v1 spec and refuses names of removed presets.

Kedro graph. Unique node names (bank.assign_linked_donor_folds); viz.plot_mock_truth out of __default__; no empty ml pipeline; cite.* tags only for scientific references and codes, plus one env.<stack> tag per node; sim.generate_views carries the citations of the per-view physics; the public Kedro-Viz is built without machine paths. Kept as they are, with the options below: the per-view chain inside one node and the inputs read outside the catalog.

Open decisions for the author (options and effects; the first is the recommendation):

  1. Closure deficit (about 15 per cent with a realistic background). (a) Rerun the closure grid on release 1.1.x (about 3 node-hours), then decide; (b) first replace the fit’s typical-EPIC background seed by each observation’s own particle background (S9 / F5), then rerun; (c) carry the measured deficit as a calibration by (z, profile, S/N) and divide it out. (a) tells whether the release 1.1 background model changed the deficit; (b) attacks the likely cause but changes the fit and the real masses; (c) needs many more cells than 24 per mode to be a calibration.

  2. Gate P of the replica list. (a) Use the matched draw K’ = 5 of the maximal list for every population statistic and keep the replica list for the realism pairing; (b) widen the epoch window (epoch_candidates = 3, epoch_max_dz = 0.25: coverage 0.972, mass still fails); (c) weight the replica views to the real mass distribution. (a) passes the gate without new generation; (b) changes the committed list and every view id after it; (c) keeps the views but adds weights to every statistic.

  3. Off-axis PSF of the cluster photons. (a) An energy- and angle-dependent King PSF behind a new hashed switch instrument.psf_model (default the on-axis King, so no hash moves), about 1-2 days with tests, then a node job of generations and fits to measure the effect by core offset and theta500; (b) the CCF PSF images as sampling kernels (about 3 days); (c) carry it as an unquantified systematic. (a) measures the asymmetry the review asks for at the lowest cost.

  4. Sky background. (a) Measure the 0.5-1 keV vignetted field level of every donor and of its replica with the existing decomposition (login node) and, if they differ, scale the foreground per donor behind a hashed switch; (b) scale with the ROSAT R45 level of the field (needs the RASS maps); (c) keep the fixed xmm_simulator sky and state it. (a) uses the donors’ own data and the code that exists.

  5. Kedro graph. (a) Keep the per-view chain in one node and the data-package inputs outside the catalog (documented in 1.1.3); (b) declare the donor tables and the fit-target tables as Kedro inputs of sim.generate_views and of the massfit pipeline, which changes node signatures and needs the end-to-end acceptance run on a node; (c) split sim.generate_views into gas, photons, instrument, photometry and truth nodes, which breaks the “Kedro writes the bytes of campaign run” contract unless the CLI is split the same way. (b) is the useful part of the review’s request at moderate cost.

  6. The fitted mocks are not count-matched (about twice the net counts of the real observation). (a) Fit a count-matched replay of each mock of the certification sample as a second arm (node time); (b) keep and state the S/N difference. (a) separates the bias from the S/N regime.

Release 1.1.3, continued: the node work (2026-10-27)¶

Superseded in part. Decision 3: since 2026-11-03 the production cluster PSF is king-energy-angle (“Independent review of the replacements, round 1”, item C). The typical-epic seed of the production fit was removed on 2026-11-03 (“No tuned factors: the last replacements”).

The author delegated the six decisions above (take the recommended option unless the evidence says otherwise) and allowed up to two nodes. What was run, found and decided:

  • A bug in the background-free views. The residual soft protons of the donor background (soft_protons = 'inout', release 1.1) were drawn whenever the donor record had a rate, also when the particle background was switched off (with_qpb = False). The clean and agnonly cells of a closure grid made with release 1.1 or later therefore held the donor’s soft protons, while the fit froze their background at zero: the first factorial run gave M_fit / M_true of 0.1-0.2 for the large clusters at z = 0.2. Fixed (simulate_instrument.soft_protons_on); production always has the particle background, so no campaign product changes. The invalid cells were deleted and regenerated (job 1889883).

  • Closure factorial on the current configuration (jobs 1889878, cancelled when the bug was found, and 1889883; 216 quick fits, 2026-10-27): median M_fit / M_true clean 1.048 / 1.044 / 1.042 at z = 0.06 / 0.2 / 0.5, background only 0.773 / 1.031 / 1.060, real 0.830 / 0.940 / 0.987; the AGN-only cells (0.94 / 0.81 / 0.83) are not a meaningful mode as the grid fits them: their background is frozen at zero although the unresolved AGN make one. The per-fit scatter is 0.08 in ln M at z = 0.06 and 0.2-0.3 at z = 0.2-0.5, several times the 0.05-0.07 of the V4 grid. 9 of the 72 cells meet the registered 1.00 +- 0.03; the gate fails. The deficit with a background sits at z = 0.06, where the fit seeds its background from the typical EPIC level (calibrated on the V4 background model); at z = 0.2-0.5 the background is seeded from the data and the real cells are within 6 per cent.

  • Decision 2 (gate P): option (a). The matched draw (K’ = 5, R = 12, dz_max 0.25) recomputed with the current code from the committed inputs passes gate P strictly (matching mass D = 0.026 against 0.067, coverage 0.978); it is the sample of every population statistic, and the replica list keeps the realism pairing. On the intrinsic hydrostatic bias the two samples differ by under 1 per cent (median 0.885 against 0.877).

  • Decision 5 (Kedro graph): option (a), kept as documented. Declaring the donor tables as inputs of sim.generate_views would give one dataset name two meanings: the donor_tables pipeline writes those datasets (in the data package for base, in the fixtures area for the acceptance runs), while the generation always reads the data package’s copy; a fixtures run of sim would then load tables that the acceptance runs have not written. Splitting the per-view chain into nodes breaks the contract that a Kedro run writes the bytes of campaign run unless the CLI is split the same way. Neither is worth the risk for a graph that the pipelines page now documents.

  • Progenitor selection (internal/ledger_tests/progenitor_bias.py): at matched M500c within each snapshot the TNG-Cluster halos have ln(M_HSE / M500c) 0.032 lower than TNG300’s (95 per cent -0.050 to -0.011; 1,564 against 708 halo-snapshots), 0.04-0.07 at z >= 0.2, 0.01-0.02 at z <= 0.1.

  • Real-against-real null of the realism test (internal/ledger_tests/realism_real_null.py): two random halves of the development donors’ real observations give AUC 0.49 +- 0.03-0.04, 95th percentile 0.53-0.56, at 194 and 242 per side.

  • The RSS probe test. Main (82064df) passes the whole unit suite on the login node (1,438 passed); the probe test failed twice on the 1.1.3 tree in a full-suite run (0.038 GB measured for a 64 MB array) and passed alone. It now allocates 256 MB; the code under test is unchanged.

  • Memory of the validation campaign. The chain job (1889893) ran out of memory at snapshot 91 with 40 generation workers and five concurrent gas loads (236 GB peak); the resubmission (1889896) runs 20 workers, three loads and eight truth workers.

  • Chains and background seed are not the cause (job 1889894, 144 fits on the 72 clean and real cells): the same cells refitted with long chains (500 + 1500 steps) move by a median d ln M = -0.002 (95 per cent -0.006 to +0.011), and refitted with each observation’s own measured background level as the seed (decision 1, option (b), massfit.bg_seed = 'donor-table') by -0.003 (-0.011 to +0.005), -0.001 at z = 0.06. Option (b) does not meet the closure target and is not switched on; it stays a hashed fit switch.

  • The Galactic column of the fit (job 1889901, closure arm v1_nhtot, 216 quick fits on the same cells): the cells are absorbed by N_H,tot = N_HI + 2 N_H2 of the donor field, and every fit used the HI4PI column. Fitting with N_H,tot (massfit.fit_galactic_nh = 'willingale13') moves the two donors with N_H,tot / N_HI of 1.88 and 1.84 from median M_fit / M_true 1.51 and 1.19 to 0.87 and 0.78, and the other seven donors by -0.06 to +0.01 in ln M. The per-fit scatter of the clean cells at z = 0.2 drops from 0.25 to 0.07 in ln M. With the total column, the clean cells give 1.02 / 0.94 / 0.97 at z = 0.06 / 0.2 / 0.5, background only 0.76 / 0.92 / 0.88 and real 0.76 / 0.87 / 0.88, the same for every donor. The scatter was the column; what remains is a deficit whenever the data hold a background. The real clusters are absorbed by the total column too, so the fit of the real data needs the same rule.

  • Sky background, memo 4 (internal/ledger_tests/sky_level_4a.py, login node, 467 views of the subset sky of campaign_v113 against the observations whose conditions they carry). The 0.5-1 keV field beyond 2 R500 is split into a vignetted and a flat part per camera. Median ln(mock / real):

    • vignetted part (the rule written before the measurement): MOS1 +0.08 +- 0.07, MOS2 -0.26 +- 0.09, PN +0.34 +- 0.06;

    • flat part: -0.25, +0.31, -0.41;

    • total level: +0.07 +- 0.03, +0.03 +- 0.03, +0.20 +- 0.04.

    The two parts move in opposite directions by similar amounts, so in this band the split is poorly constrained by the decomposition. The total is not: the MOS fields are within 7 per cent, and the pn field of the mocks is 22 per cent brighter. The rule as stated (|median| <= 0.1 for the vignetted part of every camera) fails. A per-donor scaling of the foreground is not switched on, because the quantity it would scale is the poorly determined one. The fit models the background of each image freely, so the masses do not depend on this level; the realism tests do. The pn excess is an open item for the instrument stage (its flat 0.5-1 keV part), with the measurement above.

  • Resumed views lost their observation columns. Two views of campaign_v113, resumed from the job that ran out of memory, had NaN z_obs_source, seed and conditions_obsid in the results table (checks C12 and C16): the resume path copied only the measurement columns from obs_meta.json. Fixed (generate_view.CACHED_META_COLS, test); the two rows were refilled from their obs_meta.json, the table otherwise byte-identical (backup kept beside it).

  • Decision 3 (cluster PSF): the on-axis King stays the production model (job 1889922, arm v1_psfea, 108 clean and background-only cells). The cells are generated with the King parameters interpolated in photon energy and off-axis angle (instrument.psf_model = 'king-energy-angle', at each donor’s core offset) and fitted with the on-axis King, as a real cluster is. The paired change of ln(M_fit / M_true) against the on-axis cells is +0.001 over all cells (95 per cent -0.021 to +0.019). Clean cells: -0.009, +0.016 and +0.037 at z = 0.06, 0.2 and 0.5, each with an interval that includes zero. An off-axis PSF in the fit is not needed at this precision. The switch stays available and hashed only when set.

  • The particle background’s spatial form is not the cause of the background deficit (same job, arm v1_qpbflat: the cells regenerated with the QPB flat over the detector, which is what the fit assumes, instead of the filter-wheel-closed density). Paired change over the background-only cells: +0.031, +0.046 and +0.023 at z = 0.06, 0.2 and 0.5, against deficits of 0.24, 0.08 and 0.12.

  • What the deficit with a background is, and is not (jobs 1889917 and 1889933, login analysis internal/ledger_tests/closure_bg_truth.py).

    • At z = 0.06, three changes leave M_fit / M_true of the background cells unchanged (paired, 18 cells each):

      • a 1 dex prior on the seeded background instead of 0.1 dex (arm v1_bgwide): +0.016 (bgonly) and -0.040 (real);

      • the vignetted fraction frozen at the seed (v1_vffix): -0.027 and -0.13;

      • nine optimiser starts instead of one (v1_grid): -0.009 and -0.020, so the single start is not stuck in a local optimum.

    • A background-truth view of every bgonly and real cell (arm v1_bgcell: the same instrument, background and AGN, 1e-4 of the cluster photons) was cut exactly as the fit cut the cell. Against it:

      • at z = 0.2 and 0.5 the fitted background is within 1-3 per cent of the truth in every band of the bgonly cells, so the remaining deficit of 8-12 per cent there does not come from the background level;

      • at z = 0.06 the fitted background is 15-21 per cent too low in the 0.5-4.5 keV bands. The cluster model takes that light, and the mass comes out low.

    • Why it is low at z = 0.06: the seed the fit is held to (0.1 dex prior) is the typical EPIC level, calibrated on the V4 background model, and it lies 25-37 per cent (0.28-0.47 in ln) below the release 1.1 background of these cells. The observation’s own seed (donor-table) was lower still (0.30-0.60). That was a unit error: mbproj2d multiplies the unvignetted exposure map by rho = median(expmap / expmap_novig), 0.52 below 4.5 keV and 0.34 above, and the seed’s flat part was not divided by rho. Fixed (bg_seed.donor_background_seed(rho=...), test). The production fit uses typical-epic, so no production product changes. The earlier v1_ownbg fits predate the fix; v1_ownbg2 and v1_frz use it.

  • AGN-only cells with a free background (the rule fixed in this release): at z = 0.06 median 0.966 against 0.94 with the background frozen at zero.

Design note: matching on the hydrostatic fit mass, in two passes (2026-10-27, for releases 1.1.3 and 1.2)¶

Since 2026-11-26: implemented. The pass-1 and pass-2 presets exist (config.PASS1_PRESETS, config.PASS2_PRESETS), pass 1 has been generated and fitted, and the pairing commands are tngxmm donors pair-fit-mass and pair-projections (“Pass 2 with six projections and the added observations”).

Status on 2026-10-27: proposed, not implemented; no job has been run for it. It reverses “The soft-band luminosity excess of TNG-Cluster” on the matching mass: mocks and real clusters are paired on the hydrostatic mass returned by the same X-ray fitter on both sides. Catalogue masses (Planck, ACT, lensing) and the assumed bias 0.78 leave the matching and serve only as cross-checks. No calibration constant (luminosity factor, mass-bias factor, proxy-to-fit-mass curve) enters.

What depends on the current pairing (true M500c x 0.78 against catalogue masses). Gate P and the adoption of the matched draw (decision 2), S7, the count-matching factor 0.54 of the closure arm v1_thin, the figures samples and hse_bias_truth (matched-draw curve), and the donor of every view of the validation campaign campaign_v113 (job 1889896, 908 views from the replica and target lists). Of that campaign, the per-donor sky level (memo 4) and the X-ray fit masses of its views do not depend on why a donor was chosen; the frozen realism evaluation does (it compares each mock with its paired donor) and is held. Independent of the pairing: the closure grid, the soft-proton fix, the background seed, the PSF model, the Galactic column, the progenitor test (true masses at fixed snapshot), the real-against-real null, Kedro, S8.

Masses that exist.

  • Real side: 167 observations have a fit mass (data/certify/tables/real_fits.csv, group v4, fits under tngxmm_dev/CHECKS/fits/d4/v4; V4 fit configuration, 2026-10-17); 165 converged, 106 pass the fit QC. The donor pool is 638 observations, so 471 have no fit. The fits are seeded from the 1 Mpc soft-band luminosity, never from a catalogue mass. Distribution of the 165: 6 / 31 / 48 / 38 / 12 / 20 / 11 in M_fit = 1-2 / 2-4 / 4-6 / 6-8 / 8-10 / 10-15 / > 15e14 Msun.

  • Mock side: the 3-D hydrostatic mass (Barnes et al. 2021 estimator) at R500c for every candidate, 3,258 halo-snapshots (535 halos, 2,657 (sim, halo_id) pairs; campaign_v1_full); X-ray fit masses for 43 views (development sample D1, group v4s1 and seven sensitivity groups, V4 configuration).

  • A preliminary L_X-M_fit comparison is possible now, on V4 products only: log10(L_X,1Mpc / E(z)) against log10(M_fit / 5e14 Msun), least squares with a bootstrap: real (165) slope 0.58 +- 0.07, normalisation 44.43 +- 0.02, scatter 0.22 dex; mock D1 (40) slope 0.93 +- 0.11, normalisation 44.70 +- 0.07, scatter 0.20 dex. The samples are not selected alike (D1 reaches z = 1.27, the real fits include M_fit > 15e14 Msun) and both use the V4 fitter, so this is not the test, only an indication that the mocks are about 0.26 dex brighter at fixed fit mass.

Procedure.

  1. Real side. Refit all 638 donor observations with the release 1.1.3 fitter (production fit switches, quick chains as for the mocks, SAS source lists), so both sides use one fitter version. Luminosity: L_X in the fixed 1 Mpc aperture (lx_1mpc), measured on mocks and real data by the same photometry; the catalogue-based lx_r500 is not used.

  2. Pass 1 (provisional mass). One view per candidate halo-snapshot (projection axis 0 of the ten), observed under one fixed reference observation for every halo, placed on-axis at the halo’s redshift. The reference is a real observation chosen by a stated rule, without tuning: all three cameras in full-frame modes, and the smallest summed rank distance to the donor-pool medians of effective exposure per camera, HI4PI column, N_H,tot / N_HI and the flare-filtered particle background. Its obsid and values are recorded before the run. Production fitter; the fit mass is the halo-snapshot’s matching mass.

  3. Pairing. Each halo-snapshot is paired with real observations on (ln M_fit, z) using the matched-draw machinery (dz_max 0.25 as now; the mass distance is ln M_fit on both sides). No catalogue mass, no bias factor.

  4. Pass 2. Generate the production views under the paired donors and fit them. The halo-snapshot’s matching mass becomes the median fit mass of its fitted production views. Re-pair; regenerate and refit only the halo-snapshots whose donor set changed.

  5. Convergence rule, fixed before the first run: stop when fewer than 5 per cent of the paired halo-snapshots change any donor between two iterations, or after three iterations of step 4, whichever comes first. The history (pairs changed per iteration, median |d ln M_fit| per iteration) is reported. A non-converged pairing is reported as such.

  6. Results, reported as they come out: the mock and real L_X-M_fit relations (slope, normalisation at 5e14 Msun, scatter, bootstrap intervals over halos and observations; same luminosity estimator, same fitter, the same z and mass range); cross-checks of real M_fit against Planck, ACT and lensing masses and of mock M_fit against the true M500c (the hydrostatic plus method bias). Candidate paper figure.

Galactic column: the closure grid shows that fitting with the HI4PI column while the photons are absorbed by N_H,tot biases the mass high in fields with N_H,tot / N_HI near 1.9 (closure 1.3-1.5). Pass 1 places every halo in one field and pass 2 in its donors’, so a column error would enter the two passes differently. Both sides should be fitted with the same column rule; if the closure arm v1_nhtot confirms the effect, with the total column (massfit.fit_galactic_nh = 'willingale13').

Release 1.2: six projections per halo, each under a different donor.

  • The six axes (through opposite vertices of the icosahedron) for all 3,258 halo-snapshots are 19,548 views. The cubes of half-side 4 sqrt(2) = 5.66 R500c exist (job 1889862, prod/campaign_v12_cubes, 3,258 halo-snapshots verified complete); generation needs a cylinder-size parameter separate from the cube half-side (cylinder 4 R500c).

  • Distinct donors per halo. The conditions donor (exposure, background, footprint, column) can always be distinct: six of 638. The physics donor, the real cluster matched in (M_fit, z), cannot always be. The real fits cover the mock range unevenly: 6 real fits at 1-2e14 Msun against 1,227 mock halo-snapshots there (3-D M_HSE), and 31 above 1e15 against 55. With a fixed window (dz_max 0.25 and the matched-draw mass distance) a halo gets its distinct matches up to the number available; the shortfall is filled by repeating the nearest matches, never by widening the window. Per halo the number of distinct physics donors is recorded, and the distribution is reported by mass and z. Halo-snapshots with no real observation inside the window are kept for training with conditions donors only, flagged unpaired, and excluded from every mock-against-real statistic. Whether 638 refitted observations change this is known after step 1.

  • Views in the matching. Pass 1 needs one view per halo-snapshot. In pass 2 the matching mass is the median fit of the views that are fitted (below); the six views share one halo-level pairing (a one-to-many assignment of up to six distinct physics donors), so the projections do not each move the pairing.

  • Folds and bootstrap are grouped by halo (all snapshots and all six views of a halo in one fold and one resample unit, 535 halos), and the donor folds stay linked as now (bank.assign_linked_donor_folds), so that no donor and no halo appears in both training and test.

  • Fits: pass 1 (3,258), the real refits (638), and in pass 2 two of the six views per halo-snapshot (6,516) for the matching mass and its projection scatter; the other views are not fitted unless a study needs them.

Node cost (estimates from this week’s jobs). Generation runs at about 310 views per node-hour (job 1889896: 20 workers, memory-bound). Quick fits cost about 0.75 node-hours per 100 (closure job 1889883: 216 fits in 1.6 node-hours); real observations and views with SAS source detection are taken at 1 node-hour per 100.

At one node at a time pass 1 with the real refits is about two days, the first iteration of pass 2 about five and a half days. A release 1.1.3 version with one view per halo-snapshot in pass 2 costs 11 + 33 = 44 node-hours per iteration.

Forecast of the L_X(1 Mpc)-M_fit comparison (2026-10-27). A forecast, not a result.¶

Asked by the author before the fit-mass matching runs: will the mock and observed L_X(1 Mpc)-M_fit relations agree once the planned changes are in? Computed on the login node from existing products only (internal/ledger_tests/forecast_lx_mfit.py, output tngxmm_dev/V113/forecast/v3). The forecast was checked three times by an independent read of its code and text (tngxmm_dev/V113/forecast_review/); the third found no major objection.

  • Relation: OLS of log10(L_X,1Mpc / E(z)) on log10(M_fit / 5e14 Msun). Both sides use the same L_X estimator, the same fitter and the same aperture.

  • Starting point (V4 products): 165 real fits give slope 0.58 +- 0.07 and normalisation 44.433 +- 0.018. The 40 D1 mock fits give 0.93 +- 0.11 and 44.695 +- 0.065.

  • Mock side. Each pool view (TNG-Cluster and TNG300, 8,980 views with a usable L_X) gets a proxy fit mass: its 3-D hydrostatic mass times a ratio drawn from the 5 D1 fits nearest in mass and redshift. The proxy is used only for this forecast, never as a matching input.

    • On the D1 views themselves, the proxy misses their actual fits by +0.047 +- 0.063 in normalisation. The four proxy kinds tried miss by +0.036 to +0.081.

    • The ratio is shifted by half the change of the real-mode closure from V4 to 1.1.3 (+0.02 to +0.09 in ln M).

    • The views are reweighted to the (M_fit, z) distribution of the real pool.

  • Real side.

    • The masses are corrected for the Galactic column, which moves the median by -0.018 in ln M. The correction is 3.65 per 1e22 cm^-2 of missing column, measured on the closure grid but resting on two donors.

    • The 165 fitted observations are brighter than the unfitted ones at fixed catalogue mass and z, by +0.098 +- 0.024 dex (PSZ2).

    • The line of the whole primary PSZ2 pool is therefore simulated: each unfitted observation gets an M_fit drawn from its catalogue mass and L_X residual. This lowers the normalisation by 0.109 dex (0.137 with ACT).

  • Forecast lines at 5e14 Msun:

    • real (PSZ2 pool): slope 0.82, normalisation 44.344;

    • mock: slope 0.82, normalisation 44.638;

    • offset mock - real +0.29 +- 0.08 dex, or +0.21 to +0.42 with the proxy’s closure residual put on the upper side;

    • the six variants (three proxy kinds, two pools) give +0.29 to +0.36.

  • Reading: the mocks are forecast to be about twice as bright as the real clusters at fixed fit mass. The two relations are not expected to agree within the errors (3.6 sigma). No slope forecast is made: the slopes depend on the sample distribution and on the mass noise.

  • The largest single uncertainty is the fit-mass proxy (0.063 dex), which rests on 40 D1 fits. Every known residual points upward, so +0.29 is a lower-side central value.

  • What happens to the excess on a hydrostatic axis: at fixed 3-D hydrostatic mass the pool is 0.035 dex brighter than at fixed true M500c. The excess is a property of the gas, not of the mass definition.

  • Not modelled:

    • whether M_fit depends on its L_X seed (S6 has not been run);

    • archive selection of the real pool;

    • the 12-use cap per donor and the six projections of the matched draw;

    • a larger M_fit scatter for the unfitted (fainter) observations, which would raise the offset.

Fitter freeze, proposed for sign-off (2026-10-27)¶

Superseded 2026-11-03 (“No tuned factors: the last replacements”): the fit column is the total Galactic column (massfit.fit_galactic_nh = 'willingale13'), and the typical-epic background seed is replaced by the ESAS background (massfit.qpb_model = 'esas-fwc-corners').

The settings below are proposed as the fitter for the real refits and pass 1 of the fit-mass matching. Two values differ from the v1 preset; changing them moves the v1 spec hash, so they go in with the release that runs the refits. Evidence comes from the release 1.1.3 closure grid: 216 cells (M500 = 2, 4, 8e14 Msun; z = 0.06, 0.2, 0.5; cc and ncc; four data modes; three seeds), one arm per job, every arm on the same cells.

Galactic column of the fit

total, N_HI + 2 N_H2 (Willingale et al. 2013) of the field (massfit.fit_galactic_nh = 'willingale13'; v1: HI4PI)

the mocks and the real clusters are absorbed by it; with HI4PI the two donors with N_H,tot / N_HI = 1.84-1.88 give M_fit / M_true 1.19-1.51, with the total column 0.78-0.87 like the others; clean-cell scatter at z = 0.2 0.25 -> 0.07 in ln M (job 1889901)

background seed where no annulus beyond 2 R500 is on the detector

the observation’s own measured flat level (massfit.bg_seed = 'donor-table', with the unit fix of this release; v1: typical-epic)

the typical level was calibrated on the V4 background and lies 25-37 per cent below the release 1.1 background; the own level is a measurement of the observation, no table fitted to mocks (its vignetted part is still the typical one); closure effect small (z = 0.06 bgonly 0.759 -> 0.775)

Against the registered rule (1.00 +- 0.03 in every cell), 13 of the 72 cells pass. The test fails, and it is reported as it is.

What is known about the remaining deficit.

  • z = 0.06: the fitted background is about 20 per cent too low in every camera and band below 4.5 keV, at every radius, and the cluster model takes that light. The fit is not held to a wrong seed: the corrected own seed is 15-25 per cent above the truth. The data alone prefer the low background, because a cluster that fills the field and a flat background cannot be told apart there. This is a property of a single pointing of a cluster larger than the field, not a setting, hence the QC flag.

  • z = 0.2 and 0.5: the fitted background is within 1-3 per cent of the truth in every band, yet M_fit / M_true is 0.86-0.91 with a background against 0.95-1.00 without one. None of the following is the cause:

    • the background level;

    • the particle background shape;

    • the PSF;

    • the chains;

    • the start;

    • the metallicity prior;

    • the column.

    The per-fit scatter is about 0.1 in ln M, so the median of 18 fits per cell group is known to about 0.03.

Left open.

  • The cause of the 9-14 per cent deficit with a background at z >= 0.2.

  • The S/N dependence of the closure: the thinned arm was not rerun on the release 1.1.3 grid.

  • The pn 0.5-1 keV field level of the mocks: +0.20 in ln against the real observations.

  • Whether the real refits use quick chains (proposed, as the mocks).

The real refits (638 observations, prepared in tngxmm_dev/V113/realfit_113) and pass 1 wait for the author’s sign-off of this section.

Counts check of the fitter freeze: the rule, written before the run (2026-10-27)¶

The author’s sign-off of the fitter freeze is conditional on this check.

  • Run: closure arm v1_frzthin, the proposed freeze settings (arm v1_frz) on the 216 cells regenerated with each absorbed cluster photon kept with probability 0.54. Background, AGN and instrument are unchanged, and the random streams are those of the unthinned cells.

  • Statistic: for each data mode and redshift (12 groups of 18 cells), the median over the cells of the paired difference d = ln(M_fit / M_true)_thinned - ln(M_fit / M_true)_v1_frz, with a 95 per cent bootstrap interval over cells (4,000 resamples).

  • Rule: the deficit does not depend on counts if, in each of the six groups with a background (bgonly and real, at z = 0.06, 0.2 and 0.5), |median d| <= 0.05 and the 95 per cent interval includes zero. In addition, no group of the twelve may have |median d| > 0.10.

  • The clean and AGN-only groups are reported, but enter only through the 0.10 limit.

  • The tolerance 0.05 is about 1.5 times the standard error of a median of 18 fits with a per-fit scatter of 0.1 in ln M.

Two of the six background groups break the rule:

  • background only at z = 0.5: the thinned cells give 16 per cent less mass, and the interval excludes zero;

  • real at z = 0.06: +0.053, just outside the 0.05 tolerance, with an interval that includes zero.

The freeze is therefore not signed off. The cells without a background do not depend on the counts (|d| <= 0.02).
